in the following
way. Let
A
be an object of
\mathscr{M}(X)
. We set:

C^{i}(A)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}0&\mbox{ if }i\geqslant 1\\
A&\mbox{ if }i=0\\
\bigoplus_{I\subset\{1,\dots,r\},|J|=-i}(j_{J})^{\mathscr{M}}_{!}(j_{J})^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}A&\mbox{ if }i\leqslant-1.\end{array}\right.

The differential of
C^{\bullet}(A)
is an alternating sum of maps given by

2.5
. Then, the left adjoint of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{Z}(\mathscr{M}(X))\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X))
is
the functor sending
A^{\bullet}
to the total complex of
C^{\bullet}(A^{\bullet})
.
∎

Let
Z
be a closed immersion such that the open immersion
j:U\hookrightarrow X
of the complement of
Z
in
X
is affine. It follows from the proof of
4.2
that we have a canonical exact triangle

j^{\mathscr{M}}_{!}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Id}\rightarrow i^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\xrightarrow{+1}.

Moreover the diagram

(4.2)

\textstyle{{j^{\mathscr{P}}_{!}j^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta^{\mathscr{M}}_{j}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{i^{\mathscr{P}}_{*}i^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{i}^{\mathscr{M}}}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\textstyle{{j^{\mathscr{P}}_{!}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{U}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho^{\mathscr{M}}_{j}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{i^{\mathscr{P}}_{*}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Z}i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\scriptstyle{(\gamma_{i}^{\mathscr{M}})^{-1}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}j^{\mathscr{M}}_{!}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}i^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}

is commutative (the morphisms in the second row are those obtained by adjunction from
\theta_{i}^{\mathscr{M}}
and the inverse of
\theta^{\mathscr{M}}_{j}
).

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
i:Z\rightarrow X
be a closed immersion. Then, the natural transformation

\theta^{\mathscr{M}}_{i}:i^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}\rightarrow\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Z}\circ i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}

is invertible.

Proof.

The statement is local on
X
, so we may assume that
X
is affine.
Then, as in the proof of
4.1
, we can write

i=i_{r}\circ\ldots\circ i_{1}
, where each
i_{s}
is a closed immersion
with affine complement.
Using the compatibility of the
2
-morphisms
\theta^{\mathscr{M}}_{i}
with the composition of morphisms in
{}^{\mathrm{Imm}}(\mathrm{Sch}/k)
we may assume that the open immersion
j:U\hookrightarrow X
of the complement of
Z
in
X
is affine. Then, our assertion follows from (
4.2
) and the conservativity of the functor
i_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}
.
∎

4.2.
Gluing of the pullback
2
-functors

Let us fix a global left adjoint
{}^{\mathrm{Imm}}\mathsf{H}^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
of
{}^{\mathrm{Imm}}\mathsf{H}_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}
.
To be able to glue the
2
-functors
{}^{\mathrm{Imm}}\mathsf{H}^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
and
{}^{\mathrm{Liss}}\mathsf{H}^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
using
[
Ayo07a
, Théorème 1.3.1]
, it suffices to construct, for every commutative square

(4.3)

\textstyle{{X^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{Y^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{Y}

such that
i,i^{\prime}
are closed immersions and
f,f^{\prime}
smooth morphisms, a
2
-isomorphism

(4.4)

i^{\prime*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}f^{\prime*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}

and prove that these
2
-isomorphisms define an exchange structure, that is, they are compatible with the horizontal and vertical composition of commutative squares (see
[
Ayo07a
, Définition 1.2.1]
).

dg-enhancements

For the general theory of dg categories we refer to Drinfeld’s
[
Dri04
]
, Keller’s
[
Kel94
,
Kel06
]
or Toën’s
[
Tofrm[o]–1
]
.
Let
\matheusm A
be an abelian category. We denote by
\matheusm{C}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\matheusm A)
the dg category of bounded complexes of objects of
\matheusm A
and by
\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\matheusm A)
the dg quotient of
\matheusm{C}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\matheusm A)
by the subcategory of acyclic bounded complexes (for a simple construction of the dg quotient see
[
Dri04
, §3.1]
). The bounded derived category
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\matheusm A)
of
\matheusm A
is the homotopy category of the dg category
\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\matheusm A)
. We let
\mathrm{rep}(\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\matheusm A),\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\matheusm B))

be the category of dg quasi-functors from
\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\matheusm A)
to
\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\matheusm B)
(this category is denoted by
\mathcal{T}(\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\matheusm A),\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\matheusm B))
in Vologodsky’s paper
[
Vol10
]
). Let us recall the following particular case of
[
Vol10
, Theorem 1]
.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
\matheusm A,\matheusm B
be abelian categories and
F,G\in r(\matheusm A,\matheusm B)
be dg quasi-functors. Assume that the induced triangulated functors
F,G:\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\matheusm A)\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\matheusm B)
are
t
-exact for the classical
t
-structures.
Then
F,G
are respectively canonically isomorphic to the functors induced by the exact functors
H^{0}F:\matheusm A\rightarrow\matheusm B,H^{0}G:\matheusm A\rightarrow\matheusm B
and the canonical map

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{rep}(\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\matheusm A),\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\matheusm B))}(F,G)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Fct}(\matheusm A,\matheusm B)}(H^{0}F,H^{0}G)

is an isomorphism.

A triangulated functor
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\matheusm A)\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\matheusm B)
is said to be dg enhanced if it is induced by some dg quasi-functor in
\mathrm{rep}(\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\matheusm A),\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\matheusm B))
. Note that a composition of dg enhanced functors is also dg enhanced.

Remark 4.5
.

Let
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
be a closed immersion and
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a smooth morphism of quasi-projective
k
-varieties. By construction the triangulated functors
i^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}
and
f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
are dg enhanced. This is also the case of the triangulated functor

i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}:\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X))\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(Z)).

Indeed, let
j:U\hookrightarrow X
be the open immersion of the complement of
Z
in
X
and fix a finite open covering of
U
by affine open subsets. Let
\matheusm D^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg},Z}(\mathscr{M}(X))
be the dg full subcategory of
\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathscr{M}(X))
formed by the complexes that belongs to
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{Z}(\mathscr{M}(X))
. We have then dg-functors

(4.5)

\textstyle{{\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathscr{M}(Z))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}}
\textstyle{{\matheusm D^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg},Z}(\mathscr{M}(X))}}
\textstyle{{\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathscr{M}(X))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{C^{\bullet}}

where
C^{\bullet}
is the dg functor constructed (using the given open covering of
U
by affine open subsets) in the proof of
4.2
.
Since the dg-functor on the left is a quasi-equivalence, the diagram (
4.5
) defines a quasi-functor from
\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathscr{M}(X))
to
\matheusm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{dg}}(\mathscr{M}(Z))
that induces the triangulated functor
i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
.

Gluing of the
2
-functors

Let us now start with the construction of the
2
-isomorphisms (
4.4
).

Step 1:
When the square (
4.3
) is cartesian the
2
-isomorphism (
4.4
) is obtained
by considering the exchange structure
{}^{\mathscr{M}}Ex^{*}_{*}
on the pair
({}^{\mathrm{Imm}}\mathsf{H}_{*}^{\mathscr{M}},{}^{\mathrm{Liss}}\mathsf{H}^{*}_{\mathscr{M}})

obtained in
2.3
(in this exchange structure, all squares are cartesian). By applying
[
Ayo07a
, Proposition 1.2.5]
, we get an exchange structure
{}^{\mathscr{M}}Ex^{**}
on the pair
({}^{\mathrm{Imm}}\mathsf{H}^{*}_{\mathscr{M}},{}^{\mathrm{Liss}}\mathsf{H}^{*}_{\mathscr{M}})
for the class of cartesian squares (
4.3
). The uniqueness in loc.cit. implies that this exchange structure lifts the trivial exchange structure on
({}^{\mathrm{Imm}}\mathsf{H}^{*}_{\mathscr{P}},{}^{\mathrm{Liss}}\mathsf{H}^{*}_{\mathscr{P}})
given by the connection
2
-isomorphisms of the
2
-functor
\mathsf{H}^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}
. In particular, the conservativity of the functors
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}:\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X))\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{P}(X))
implies that
{}^{\mathscr{M}}Ex^{**}
is an iso-exchange.

Step 2:
Let us consider a commutative triangle

(4.6)

\textstyle{{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{S}

in which
i
is a closed immersion and
f,g
are smooth morphisms. As preparation for the construction of the
2
-isomorphism (
4.4
), we first construct a
2
-isomorphism

(4.7)

i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow g^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}.

To do this, observe that, if
d
is the relative dimension of
g
, then the triangulated functors
i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d]
and
g^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d]
are
t
-exact for the classical
t
-structures. This is a vanishing statement that can be checked after application of the functor
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}
and, for perverse sheaves, it follows from
[
BBD82
, 4.2.4]
since
g^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}
and
i^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}
are isomorphic. Moreover both functors are dg enhanced by
4.5
.

By
4.4
, to construct (
4.7
), it is enough to construct a
2
-isomorphism

(4.8)

i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d]\rightarrow g^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d]

where both functors are exact functors from
\mathscr{M}(S)
to
\mathscr{M}(X)
. Therefore, it suffices to prove the following proposition.

Proposition 4.6
.

Consider the commutative diagram
(
4.6
).
Let
A
be an object in
\mathscr{M}(S)
and let
K
be its underlying perverse sheaf. Then, the canonical morphism of perverse sheaves
i^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}[d](K)\rightarrow g^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}[d](K)

lies in the image of the injective morphism

(4.9)

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{M}(X)}(i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d](A),g^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d](A))\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{P}(X)}(i^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}[d](K),g^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}[d](K)).

Remark 4.7
.

Note that the map (
4.9
) is obtained via the functor
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}
using the invertible natural transformations
f^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{S}\rightarrow\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
,
g^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{S}\rightarrow\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}\circ g^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
and
i^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}\rightarrow\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}\circ i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
which have been previously constructed.

Proof.

Step (a).
Consider a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{X^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\scriptstyle{v}
\textstyle{{Y^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{S}

where
i
is a closed immersion,
f,g
are smooth morphisms and
u
is an étale morphism. By step 1, we have a natural transformation
i^{\prime*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ u^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow v^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
that lifts the corresponding natural transformation in the derived category of perverse sheaves. Assume the proposition true for the diagram (
4.6
). Then, the morphism
i^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}[d](K)\rightarrow g^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}[d](K)
lifts to a morphism
i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d](A)\rightarrow g^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d](A)
. By applying
v^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
to this lift we obtain a morphism

i^{\prime*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ f^{\prime*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d](A)\rightarrow g^{\prime*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d](A)

that lifts the morphism
i^{\prime*}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ f^{\prime*}_{\mathscr{P}}[d](K)\rightarrow g^{\prime*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d](K)
.
This shows, in particular, that if the proposition is true for the diagram (
4.6
) then it is also true for the diagram

\textstyle{{X^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{Y^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{S.}

Step (b).
Let
\mathscr{Y}=(Y_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in I}
be a finite Zariski open covering of
Y
and consider for every
\alpha\in I
the commutative diagram

\textstyle{{X_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{g_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\scriptstyle{v_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{{Y_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{f_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{S}

where
u_{\alpha}
is the open immersion of
Y_{\alpha}
in
Y
. Note that the canonical morphism of perverse sheaves
i^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}[d](K)\rightarrow g^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}[d](K)
is obtained by gluing the morphism
i^{*}_{\alpha,\mathscr{P}}\circ f^{*}_{\alpha,\mathscr{P}}[d](K)\rightarrow g^{*}_{\alpha,\mathscr{P}}[d](K)
along the Zariski open covering
\mathscr{X}=(X_{\alpha})_{\alpha\in I}

of
X
. Hence it follows from step (a) and
2.7
that the proposition is true for the diagram (
4.6
) if and only if it is true for the diagrams

\textstyle{{X_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{g_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{{Y_{\alpha}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\alpha}}
\textstyle{S.}

Step (c).
By step (b) the problem is local on
Y
for the Zariski topology. Since both
Y
and
X
are smooth over
S
, we may assume that there exists a cartesian square

\textstyle{{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{g}
\scriptstyle{\square}
\scriptstyle{v}
\textstyle{{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{u}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}^{d}_{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}^{d+c}_{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S}

where
u
is an étale morphism. Using step (a) and induction, we are reduced to proving the proposition in the case

\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}^{d}_{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}^{d+1}_{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{S}

where
p
and
\pi
are the projections and
s
is the zero section. By considering the factorization

\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}^{d}_{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}^{d+1}_{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}^{d}_{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{S}

and observing that the functors
\pi^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d]
,
\pi^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}[d]
are exact, we may further assume
d=0
.

Step (d).

It remains to prove the proposition in the case of the diagram

\textstyle{{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{S}

where
s
is the zero section and
p
is the projection.
Let
f:\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}=\mathbf{A}^{1}\times S\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}
be the first projection,
a:\mathbf{G}_{m}\times S\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}\times S
be the inclusion. We set
q=p\circ a
. Given a motive
B\in\mathscr{M}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S})
, consider the connecting morphism
B\rightarrow s^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}_{f}(a^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}(B))[1]

in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}))
obtained from the exact sequence (
3.20
). By adjunction, we get a morphism
s^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}(B)\rightarrow{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}_{f}(a^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}B)[1]

in the category
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(S))
. Taking
B
to be the perverse motive
B=p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[1]A
, we get after a shift a morphism

s^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}(A)\rightarrow{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}_{f}(q^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[1](A))

in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(S))
. As both objects are concentrated in degree zero, the above morphism is actually a morphism in the abelian category
\mathscr{M}(S)
. Moreover, it is an isomorphism since it is on the underlying perverse sheaves. Moreover, we know that the square

\textstyle{{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{M}(S)}({}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}_{f}(q^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[1](A)),A)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{P}(S)}({}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{P}}_{f}(q^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}[1](K)),K)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{M}(S)}(s^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}(A),A)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{P}(S)}(s^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ p^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}(K),K)}

is commutative. Hence, to conclude, it suffices to show that the canonical morphism of perverse sheaves

(4.10)

{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{P}}_{f}(q^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}[1](K))\rightarrow K

lifts to a morphism
{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}_{f}(q^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[1](A))\rightarrow A
in the abelian category
\mathscr{M}(S)
. By construction of the exact functors
{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}_{f}^{\mathscr{M}}
and
q^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[1]
, this is an application of the property
P2
, since (
4.10
) is the Betti realization of a natural transformation

\mathsf{Log}_{f}(q^{*}(-))\rightarrow\mathrm{Id}

in the triangulated category of étale motives on
S
.
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Consider a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{h}
\textstyle{{Y}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{S}

in which
i,s
are closed immersions and
f,g,h
are smooth morphisms. Then, the diagram

\textstyle{{i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ s^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ g^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{h^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}}
\textstyle{{(s\circ i)^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

is commutative.

Proof.

The lemma follows from the analogous statement for perverse sheaves. Indeed, let
d
be the relative dimension of
h
. It suffices to show that the diagram

\textstyle{{i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ s^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ g^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{h^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d]}
\textstyle{{(s\circ i)^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[d]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

is commutative. Since all functors in this diagram are dg enhanced and
t
-exact for the classical

-structures, by
4.4
it suffices to check the commutativity of the diagram induced on the hearts. This can be checked on the underlying perverse sheaves.
∎

.

Proposition 4.11
.

The

-isomorphisms (

) define an exchange structure i.e., they are compatible with the horizontal and vertical compositions of commutative squares.

Proof.

. Consider a commutative diagram

5.
Main theorem

In
3.5
, we have shown that the unipotent nearby and vanishing cycles functors can be defined at the level of perverse Nori motives.

Our goal is to prove that the four operations (
0.1
) can be lifted to the derived categories of perverse Nori motives.
To obtain these various functors

(5.1)

\textstyle{{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(Y))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{\mathscr{M}}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{f_{\mathscr{M}}^{!}}
\textstyle{{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{!}^{\mathscr{M}}}

(and their compatibility relations) with the least amount of effort, we have chosen to follow Ayoub’s approach developed in
[
Ayo07a
]
around the notion
of stable homotopical
2
-functor, which encompasses in a small package all the ingredients needed to build the rest of the formalism.

5.1.
Statement of the theorem

As before,
(\mathrm{Sch}/k)
denotes the category of quasi-projective
k
-varieties. Recall that a contravariant
2
-functor

\mathsf{H}^{*}:(\mathrm{Sch}/k)\rightarrow\mathfrak{TR}

is a called a stable homotopical
2
-functor (see
[
Ayo07a
, Définition 1.4.1]
) when the following six properties are satisfied.

(1)

\mathsf{H}(\varnothing)=0
(that is,
\mathsf{H}(\varnothing)
is the trivial triangulated category).

(2)

For every morphism
f:X\rightarrow Y
in
(\mathrm{Sch}/k)
, the functor
f^{*}:\mathsf{H}(Y)\rightarrow\mathsf{H}(X)
admits a right adjoint. Furthermore for every immersion
i
the counit
i^{*}i_{*}\rightarrow\mathrm{Id}
is invertible.

(3)

For every smooth morphism
f:X\rightarrow Y
in
(\mathrm{Sch}/k)
, the functor
f^{*}:\mathsf{H}(Y)\rightarrow\mathsf{H}(X)
admits a left adjoint
f_{\sharp}
. Furthermore, for every cartesian square

\textstyle{{X^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{Y^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{Y}

with
f
smooth, the exchange
2
-morphism
f^{\prime}_{\sharp}g^{\prime*}\rightarrow g^{*}f_{\sharp}
is invertible.

(4)

If
j:U\rightarrow X
is an open immersion in
(\mathrm{Sch}/k)
and
i:Z\rightarrow X
is the closed immersion of the complement, then the pair
(j^{*},i^{*})
is conservative.

(5)

If
p:\mathbf{A}^{1}_{X}\rightarrow X
is the canonical projection, then the unit morphism
\mathrm{Id}\rightarrow p_{*}p^{*}
is invertible.

(6)

If
s
is the zero section of the canonical projection
p:\mathbf{A}^{1}_{X}\rightarrow X
, then
p_{\sharp}s_{*}:\mathsf{H}(X)\rightarrow\mathsf{H}(X)
is an equivalence of categories.

The main theorem of
[
Ayo07a
]
says that these data can be expanded into a complete formalism of the four operations (see
[
Ayo07a
, Scholie 1.4.2]
).

Theorem 5.1
.

The contravariant
2
-functor
\mathsf{H}^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
constructed in
4

is a stable homotopical
2
-functor is the sense of
[
Ayo07a
, Définition 1.4.1]
, and
(\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}},\theta^{\mathscr{M}})
is a morphism of stable homotopical
2
-functors.

In particular, we can apply
[
Ayo07a
, Scholie 1.4.2]
to get the functors (
5.1
). The next subsection is devoted to the proof of
5.1
, and the reader will find some applications of the main theorem in
5.4
.

5.2.
Proof of the main theorem (
5.1
)

We start by showing the existence of the direct image functor. The most important step is the proof of the existence of the direct image by the projection of the affine line
\mathbf{A}^{1}_{Y}
onto its base
Y
.

Proposition 5.2
.

For every morphism
f:X\rightarrow Y
in
(\mathrm{Sch}/k)
, the functor

f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}:\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(Y))\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X))

admits a right adjoint
f_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}
. Moreover

(1)

if
i:Z\rightarrow X
is a closed immersion, the counit of the adjunction
i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}i_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Id}
is invertible;

(2)

the natural transformation

\gamma_{f}^{\mathscr{M}}:\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}f^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}\rightarrow f^{\mathscr{P}}_{*}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X},

obtained from
\theta^{\mathscr{M}}_{f}
by adjunction, is invertible;

(3)

if
p:\mathbf{A}^{1}_{X}\rightarrow X
is the canonical projection, then the unit morphism
\mathrm{Id}\rightarrow p^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
is invertible.

Proof.

In the proof, all products are fiber products over the base field
k
and
\mathbf{A}^{1}
is the affine line over
k
.

Step 1
:
Suppose first that
f
is a closed immersion. Then

f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
admits
f_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}
as a right adjoint by construction of
f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
,
we know point (2) by
4.3
,
and point (1) is true by (2) and by conservativity of

\mathsf{rat}_{X}^{\mathscr{M}}
.

Step 2
:
Now we consider the case where
f
is the
projection morphism
p:X:=\mathbf{A}^{1}_{Y}\rightarrow Y
. As before, if we can
prove that
p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
admits a right adjoint satisfying (2),
then point
(3) will follow automatically.

We consider the following commutative diagram:

\textstyle{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{2}}
\scriptstyle{q_{1}}
\textstyle{U\times Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{i}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Id}}

where
q_{1}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\times p
,
q_{2}
is the product of the projection
\mathbf{A}^{1}\rightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}k
and of
\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y}
,

i
is the product of the diagonal morphism of
\mathbf{A}^{1}
and of

\mathrm{Id}_{Y}
, and
j
is the complementary open inclusion.
We also denote by
s:Y\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y
the zero section of
p
.
By the smooth base change theorem (or a direct calculation), the base
change map
p^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}p_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}
is an isomorphism,
so we get a functorial isomorphism
p_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}\simeq s^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}p_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow s_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}
.

Let
K
be a perverse sheaf on
Y
. Then
L:=q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}K[1]
is perverse, and we
have
i_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}L=K[1]
, so we get an exact sequence of perverse sheaves on

\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y
:

0\rightarrow i^{\mathscr{P}}_{*}K\rightarrow j^{\mathscr{P}}_{!}j_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}L\rightarrow L\rightarrow 0.

Applying the functor
q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}
and using the fact that
q_{1}\circ i=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y}
, we get an exact triangle:

q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}K\rightarrow K\rightarrow q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}j_{!}^{\mathscr{P}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}L\xrightarrow{+1}.

We claim that
q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}j_{!}^{\mathscr{P}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}L
is perverse. Indeed, this
complex is concentrated in perverse degrees
-1
and
0
by

[
BBD82
, 4.1.1 & 4.2.4]
. So we just need to prove
that
M:={}^{\mathsf{p}}\mathsf{H}^{-1}q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}j_{!}^{\mathscr{P}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}L
is equal to
0
.
By
[
BBD82
, 4.2.6.2]
, the adjunction
morphism
q_{1\mathscr{P}}^{*}M[1]\rightarrow j_{!}^{\mathscr{P}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}L
is injective; we denote its quotient
by
N
. Then, as
q_{1}\circ i=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y}
and
i^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}j_{!}^{\mathscr{P}}=0
,
we have
i^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}N=M[2]
. But
i
is the complement of an open affine
embedding, so
i^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}
is of perverse cohomological amplitude

[-1,0]
by
[
BBD82
, 4.1.10]
, hence
M=0
.

Finally, we get an exact sequence of perverse sheaves on
\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y
:

0\rightarrow{}^{\mathsf{p}}\mathsf{H}^{0}q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}K\rightarrow K\rightarrow q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}j_{!}^{\mathscr{P}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}K[1]\rightarrow{}^{\mathsf{p}}\mathsf{H}^{1}q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}K\rightarrow 0.

Consider the functors
\mathsf{F}_{\mathscr{P}},\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{P}}:\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y)\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y))
defined by

\mathsf{F}_{\mathscr{P}}(K):=K

and

\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{P}}(K):=q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}j_{!}^{\mathscr{P}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}K[1].

We have just proved that these functors are
t
-exact (of course, this is
obvious for the first one) and that there is a functorial exact
triangle

q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}\rightarrow\mathsf{F}_{\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{P}}\xrightarrow{+1}.

The functors
\mathsf{F}_{\mathscr{P}}
and
\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{P}}
are defined in terms of the four operations. The existence of these operations in the categories

\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(-)
and the compatibility of the Betti realization with the four operations (see
[
Ayo10
, Théorème 3.19]
), imply by the universal property of the categories of perverse motives that there exist:

•

two exact functors

\mathsf{F}_{\mathscr{M}},\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{M}}:\mathscr{M}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y)\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y),

•

a natural transformation
\mathsf{F}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{M}}
, and

•

two invertible natural transformations
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y}\circ\mathsf{F}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow\mathsf{F}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y}

and
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y}\circ\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y}

such that the diagram

\textstyle{{\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y}\circ\mathsf{F}_{\mathscr{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y}\circ\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{F}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y}}

is commutative.

Given a complex
M^{\bullet}
of perverse motives on
X=\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y
, let

\mathsf{H}_{\mathscr{M}}(M^{\bullet})
be the mapping fiber of the morphism

\mathsf{F}_{\mathscr{M}}(M^{\bullet})\rightarrow\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{M}}(M^{\bullet})
of complexes
of perverse motives on
X
. We get a triangulated functor

\mathsf{H}_{\mathscr{M}}:\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y))\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y)),

and
the Betti realization of
\mathsf{H}_{\mathscr{M}}
is isomorphic to

q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}q_{2}^{*\mathscr{P}}
.

We now define a functor

p^{\mathscr{M}}_{\bullet}:=s^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\mathsf{H}_{\mathscr{M}}(-):\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y))\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(Y)).

By construction of
p^{\mathscr{M}}_{\bullet}
, we have an invertible natural
transformation

\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y}p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow p_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y}.

Note also the following useful fact. We denote by
f:\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}
the first projection and by
a:\mathbf{G}_{m}\times Y\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y
the inclusion. Then applying
s^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
to the
connecting map in the exact sequence (
3.20
)
in
3.5
, we get a natural transformation

s^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}_{f}a^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[1],

whose composition with the functor
\mathsf{H}_{\mathscr{M}}
is invertible. Indeed,
we can check this last statement after applying the functors

\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}
, and then this follows from the exact triangle

q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}q_{2}^{*\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow\mathsf{F}_{\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{P}}\xrightarrow{+1}

and the fact that the composition of the natural transformation

s^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{P}}_{f}a^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}[1]
and of the functor

q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}q_{2}^{*\mathscr{P}}\simeq p^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}p_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}\simeq\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\boxtimes p_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}
is invertible. As the functor

{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}_{f}a^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
is exact, we get an
isomorphism from
p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}

to the mapping cone of the morphism of exact
functors
{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}_{f}a^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ\mathsf{F}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}_{f}a^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{M}}
.

Let us prove that the functor
p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}

is right adjoint to the functor
p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
.
Let
\eta_{\mathscr{P}}:\mathrm{Id}\rightarrow p^{\mathscr{P}}_{*}p_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}
and
\delta_{\mathscr{P}}:p_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}p_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Id}

be the unit and the counit
of the adjunction between
p^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}
and
p_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}
.
It suffices to lift
\eta_{\mathscr{P}}
and
\delta_{\mathscr{P}}
to natural
transformations

\eta_{\mathscr{M}}:\mathrm{Id}\rightarrow p^{\mathscr{M}}_{\bullet}p_{\mathscr{M}}^{*}
and
\delta_{\mathscr{M}}:p_{\mathscr{M}}^{*}p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Id}
such that the two natural transformations

p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\xrightarrow{p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\eta_{\mathscr{M}}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\xrightarrow{\delta_{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}

and

p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}\xrightarrow{\eta_{\mathscr{M}}p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}}p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}\xrightarrow{p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}\delta_{\mathscr{M}}}p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}

are isomorphisms and the first one is the identity (see Section 3.1 of M. Saito’s
[
Sai06
]
).
Note that the fact that these natural transformations are isomorphisms
will follow automatically from the conservativity of the functors

\mathsf{rat}_{X}^{\mathscr{M}}
.

We first construct
\eta_{\mathscr{M}}
. Let us first show that
\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}=0
.
As the functors
\mathsf{rat}_{X}^{\mathscr{M}}
are conservative, it suffices to prove that

\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ p^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}=0
. Let
k:U\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}
be the open immersion
(remember that
U
is the complement of the diagonal in
\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}
), so that
j=k\times\mathrm{Id}_{Y}
, and let
\pi:\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}
be the first projection, so that
q_{1}=\pi\times\mathrm{Id}_{Y}
. Then

\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ p^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}=q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}j_{!}^{\mathscr{P}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[1]\simeq q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}((k_{!}^{\mathscr{P}}\mathbf{Q}_{U})\boxtimes-)[1]\simeq(\pi^{\mathscr{P}}_{*}k^{\mathscr{P}}_{!}\mathbf{Q}_{U})\boxtimes(-)[1],

so it suffices to show that

\pi_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}k_{!}^{\mathscr{P}}\mathbf{Q}_{U}=0.

Let
\Delta:\mathbf{A}^{1}\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}
be the diagonal embedding. Then we
have an exact triangle

k_{!}\mathbf{Q}_{U}\rightarrow\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}}\rightarrow\Delta_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\xrightarrow{+1},

so, applying
\pi_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}
, we get an exact triangle

\pi_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}k_{!}^{\mathscr{P}}\mathbf{Q}_{U}\rightarrow\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Id}}\mathbf{Q}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\xrightarrow{+1},

and this implies the desired result.

Now that we know that
\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}=0
, we get
\mathsf{H}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}=p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
, hence
p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}=s^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ\mathsf{H}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}=s^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
, and we take for
\eta_{\mathscr{M}}:\mathrm{Id}\rightarrow p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
the
inverse of the
connection isomorphism
s^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Id}
.

Next we construct
\delta_{\mathscr{M}}
. First we define a functor

q_{1\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}:\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y))\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y))
in the same way as
p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}
. That is, we consider the
commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{r_{2}}
\scriptstyle{r_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times U\times Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J}
\textstyle{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y}
\textstyle{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Id}}
\scriptstyle{I}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{Id}}

where
r_{1}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\times q_{1}
,
r_{2}=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\times q_{2}
,

I=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\times i
and
J=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\times j
, and
we set
t=\mathrm{Id}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\times s:\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y
.
Then the functors
\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{P}},\mathsf{G}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{P}}
from
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y))
to
itself defined by
\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{P}}=\mathrm{Id}
and
\mathsf{G}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{P}}=r_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}J_{!}^{\mathscr{P}}J^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}r_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}[1]
are
t
-exact and
we have a natural transformation
\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{P}}\rightarrow\mathsf{G}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{P}}
. As before, we can lift
these functors and transformation to endofunctors
\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow\mathsf{G}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}

of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y))
. We denote by
\mathsf{H}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}
the
mapping fiber of
\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow\mathsf{G}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}
, and we set
q_{1\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}=t^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ\mathsf{H}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}
.
Also, if we denote by
f^{\prime}:\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}
the second
projection and by
a^{\prime}
the injection of

\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{G}_{m}\times Y
into
\mathbf{A}^{1}\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y
, we get as
above an invertible natural transformation from
q_{1\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}

to the mapping cone of the morphism of exact functors

{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}_{f^{\prime}}(a^{\prime})^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}_{f^{\prime}}(a^{\prime})^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ\mathsf{G}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}
.

Let’s show that the base change isomorphism
p^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}p_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}\xrightarrow{\sim}q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}
lifts to a morphism

p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow q_{1\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}q_{2\mathscr{M}}^{*}
(which will automatically be an isomorphism).
We have invertible natural transformations

\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}\simeq q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}\circ\mathsf{F}_{\mathscr{P}}
and

\mathsf{G}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{P}}\circ q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}\simeq q_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}\circ\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{P}}
. As all the functors
involved are
t
-exact up to the same shift, the transformations lift to
natural transformations
\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ q_{2\mathscr{M}}^{*}\simeq q_{2\mathscr{M}}^{*}\circ\mathsf{F_{\mathscr{M}}}
and

\mathsf{G}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ q_{2\mathscr{M}}^{*}\simeq q_{2\mathscr{M}}^{*}\circ\mathsf{G}_{\mathscr{M}}
, and induce an
invertible natural transformation
\mathsf{H}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ q_{2\mathscr{M}}^{*}\simeq q_{2\mathscr{M}}^{*}\circ\mathsf{H}_{\mathscr{M}}
. Composing on the left with
t_{\mathscr{M}}^{*}
and using the connection
isomorphism
t_{\mathscr{M}}^{*}q_{2\mathscr{M}}^{*}\simeq p_{\mathscr{M}}^{*}s_{\mathscr{M}}^{*}
, we get
the desired isomorphism
p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}\xrightarrow{\sim}q_{1\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}q_{2\mathscr{M}}^{*}
.

Composing this isomorphism with the unit of the adjunction

(i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}},i_{*}^{\mathscr{M}})
and using the connection isomorphism
i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}q_{2\mathscr{M}}^{*}\simeq\mathrm{Id}
gives a natural transformation
p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow q_{1\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}i_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}
. It remains to show that the isomorphism

q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}i_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}\simeq\mathrm{Id}
lifts to a natural transformation

q_{1\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}i_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Id}
.
First we note that the functors

(5.2)

{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathsf{B}}_{f^{\prime}}(a^{\prime})^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}r_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}r_{2}^{*\mathscr{P}}i_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}[1]

and

{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathsf{B}}_{f^{\prime}}(a^{\prime})^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}r_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}J_{!}^{\mathscr{P}}J^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}r_{2}^{*\mathscr{P}}i_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}[1]

are
t
-exact and the counit of the adjunction
(J_{!}^{\mathscr{P}},J^{*}_{\mathscr{P}})
induces a natural transformation from the second one to the first one. Hence, the functor (
5.2
)
induces an exact
endofunctor
\mathsf{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}
of
\mathscr{M}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y)
,
together with a natural transformation

{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}(a^{\prime})^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ\mathsf{G}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ i_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}
.
But we also have an invertible natural transformation
of
t
-exact functors

\displaystyle\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{P}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y))}

\displaystyle\simeq t^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}q_{1}^{*\mathscr{P}}q_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}i_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}\mbox{ (connection isomorphisms)}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}t^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}r_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}r_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}i_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}\mbox{ (base change)}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\sim}{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathsf{B}}_{f^{\prime}}(a^{\prime})^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}r_{1*}^{\mathscr{P}}r_{2\mathscr{P}}^{*}i_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}[1]\mbox{ (by \ref{subsec:AppPerverseMotive} as above)}

and all the maps in it are defined in the categories

\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(-)
, so it induces an invertible natural transformation

\mathrm{Id}_{\mathscr{M}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y)}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathsf{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}
. Composing it with

{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}(a^{\prime})^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ\mathsf{G}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ i_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}

and using the isomorphism from
q_{1\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}
to the mapping fiber of

{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}(a^{\prime})^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ\mathsf{F}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}(a^{\prime})^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ\mathsf{G}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}
,
we finally get the desired natural
transformation
\delta_{\mathscr{M}}
:

p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}\xrightarrow{\sim}q_{1\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}q_{2}^{*\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow q_{1\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}i_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow{}^{p}\mathsf{Log}^{\mathscr{M}}(a^{\prime})^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ\mathsf{G}^{\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}\circ i_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow\mathsf{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\mathscr{M}}\simeq\mathrm{Id}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times Y))}.

Finally, we check that the natural transformation

p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\xrightarrow{p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\eta_{\mathscr{M}}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\xrightarrow{\delta_{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}

is the identity.
The two functors
p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[1]
and
p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[1]
are
exact and equal to the derived functor of their
\mathbf{H}^{0}
, and the
natural transformations

(p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\eta_{\mathscr{M}})^{-1},\delta_{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}:p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}p_{\bullet}^{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[1]\rightarrow p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}[1]

are also defined by
extending their action on the
\mathbf{H}^{0}
’s, so it suffices to
check that they are equal on these
\mathbf{H}^{0}
’s. But this follows
from the analogous result for the category of perverse sheaves.

Step 3
: We can now use the Brown Representability Theorem to see that the proposition is true more generally if
f
is the projection
p:X:=E\rightarrow Y
of a vector bundle
E
on
Y
.
Indeed, given a
k
-variety
S
, let
\mathrm{Ind}(\mathscr{M}(S))
be the abelian category of
\mathrm{Ind}
-objects of
\mathscr{M}(S)
and consider the bounded derived category
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(S))
as a full subcategory of the unbounded derived category
\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{Ind}(\mathscr{M}(S)))
(see e.g. Theorem 15.3.1 of Kashiwara–Schapira’s book
[
KS06
]
). As the morphism
p:E\rightarrow Y
is smooth, the functor
p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
extends to a triangulated functor
\mathsf{L}:\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{Ind}(\mathscr{M}(Y)))\rightarrow\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{Ind}(\mathscr{M}(E)))
. By the Brown Representability Theorem (see e.g. Theorem 4.1 of Neeman’s article
[
Nee96
]
or the
book
[
Nee01
]
by the same author), the functor
\mathsf{L}
admits a right adjoint
\mathsf{R}:\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{Ind}(\mathscr{M}(E))\rightarrow\mathrm{D}(\mathrm{Ind}(\mathscr{M}(Y)))
.

To prove that
p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
admits a right adjoint
p^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}
, it suffices to check that, given
M\in\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(E))
, the object
\mathsf{R}(M)
belongs to the subcategory
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(Y))
. This can be checked on a finite Zariski open covering of
Y
that trivializes
E
and thus follows from the case of a projection
\mathbf{A}^{1}_{Y}\rightarrow Y
proved in step 2. That
p^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}
satisfies (2) can again be checked on a finite Zariski open covering of
Y
that trivializes
E
and we conclude using step 2.

Step 4
:
By steps 1 and 3, the proposition
is true if
f
is an affine
morphism. Indeed, if
f
is affine, then we can write
f=p\circ i
, where
i
is
a closed immersion and
p:E\rightarrow Y
is a vector bundle on
Y
.

Step 5
:
We now consider the case of an arbitrary morphism
f:X\rightarrow Y
in

(\mathrm{Sch}/k)
. By Jouanolou’s trick (cf. Jouanolou’s paper
[
Jou73
]
),
there exists a vector bundle
E\rightarrow X
and an affine

E
-torsor
p:\widetilde{X}\rightarrow X
. As
p
is affine, we know the
proposition for
p
by step 3. Moreover, the unit

\mathrm{Id}\rightarrow p_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
is an isomorphism; indeed, it suffices to show
this after restricting to an open covering of
X
, so we may assume that
the morphism
p
is isomorphic to the second projection
\mathbf{A}^{n}\times X\rightarrow X
, and then the result follows from point (3) of the proposition.
As the unit of the adjunction
(p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}},p_{*}^{\mathscr{M}})
is an isomorphism, the
left adjoint
p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
is fully faithful.

Let
g=f\circ p
. As

\widetilde{X}
is affine, the morphism
g
is affine. Also, we show
as before that the unit
\mathrm{Id}\rightarrow p_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}
is an isomorphism, so
we get an isomorphism
f_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}\xrightarrow{\sim}f_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}p_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\simeq g_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}
. We set
f_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}=g_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
; by the calculation we
just did, this satisfies condition (2). It remains to show that

f_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}
is right adjoint to
f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
. Let
K\in\Ob\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(Y)
and

L\in\Ob\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(X)
. Then we have isomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(Y)}(K,f_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}L)=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(Y)}(K,g_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}L)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(\widetilde{X})}(g^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}K,p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}L)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(\widetilde{X})}(p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}K,p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}L),

and the last group is isomorphic to
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(X)}(f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}K,L)
by the
full faithfulness of
p^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}
.
∎

Proposition 5.3
.

For every smooth morphism
f:X\rightarrow Y
in
(\mathrm{Sch}/k)
the functor

f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}:\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(Y))\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X))

admits a left adjoint
f_{\sharp}
. Moreover

(1)

the natural transformation

f^{\mathscr{P}}_{\sharp}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}\rightarrow\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}f^{\mathscr{M}}_{\sharp}

obtained from
\theta^{\mathscr{M}}_{f}
by adjunction, is invertible;

(2)

for every cartesian square

\textstyle{{X^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{{Y^{\prime}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{Y}

with
f
smooth, the exchange
2
-morphism
f^{\prime\mathscr{M}}_{\sharp}g^{\prime*}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow g^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}f^{\mathscr{M}}_{\sharp}
is invertible.

Proof.

The assertion (2) is an immediate consequence of (1) since the functor
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}
is conservative.

We deduce the proposition from
5.2
using Verdier duality. Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a smooth morphisms of relative dimension
d
. Note that
f^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}
has a left adjoint given by

f_{\sharp}^{\mathscr{P}}:=\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{P}}_{Y}f^{\mathscr{P}}_{*}(-d)[-2d]\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{P}}_{X}.

Therefore we similarly set
f_{\sharp}^{\mathscr{M}}:=\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}f^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}(-d)[-2d]\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}
.

Let
A
be an object in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X))
and
B
be an object in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(Y))
. Then,
5.2
and
2.6
provide isomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}(f_{\sharp}^{\mathscr{M}}A,B)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}(\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}B,f^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}(-d)[-2d]\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}A)\simeq\mathrm{Hom}(f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}(d)[2d]\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}B,\mathbb{D}_{X}^{\mathscr{M}}A)

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Hom}(A,\mathbb{D}_{X}^{\mathscr{M}}f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}(d)[2d]\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}B)\simeq\mathrm{Hom}(A,f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}B).

This shows that
(f_{\sharp}^{\mathscr{M}},f_{\mathscr{M}}^{*})
form a pair of adjoint functors.
Note that the counit
{}^{\mathscr{M}}\delta^{*}_{\sharp}
of the adjunction is given by the composition

(5.3)

f^{\mathscr{M}}_{\sharp}f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{f}^{\mathscr{M}}}\mathbb{D}_{Y}^{\mathscr{M}}f^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\mathbb{D}_{Y}^{\mathscr{M}}\xrightarrow{{}^{\mathscr{M}}\eta^{*}_{*}}(\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y})^{2}\xrightarrow{(\varepsilon_{Y}^{\mathscr{M}})^{-1}}\mathrm{Id}

and the unit
{}^{\mathscr{M}}\eta^{*}_{\sharp}
by the composition

(5.4)

\mathrm{Id}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}}(\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X})^{2}\xrightarrow{{}^{\mathscr{M}}\delta^{*}_{*}}\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}f^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{f}^{\mathscr{M}}}f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}f_{\sharp}^{\mathscr{M}}.

To show that the morphism

f^{\mathscr{P}}_{\sharp}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}\xrightarrow{{}^{\mathscr{M}}\eta^{*}_{\sharp}}f^{\mathscr{P}}_{\sharp}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}f^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}f^{\mathscr{M}}_{\sharp}\xrightarrow{(\theta^{\mathscr{M}}_{f})^{-1}}f^{\mathscr{P}}_{\sharp}f^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}f^{\mathscr{M}}_{\sharp}\xrightarrow{{}^{\mathscr{P}}\delta^{*}_{\sharp}}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}f^{\mathscr{M}}_{\sharp}

is invertible, it is enough to check that it is equal to the morphism

\textstyle{{f^{\mathscr{P}}_{\sharp}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\nu^{\mathscr{M}}_{X})^{-1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{P}}_{Y}f^{\mathscr{P}}_{*}(-d)[-2d]\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{f}^{\mathscr{M}}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{P}}_{Y}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}f^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}(-d)[-2d]\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{Y}^{\mathscr{M}}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}f^{\mathscr{M}}_{\sharp}}

where
\gamma_{f}^{\mathscr{M}}
is the invertible natural transformation of
5.2
. Using the expressions of
{}^{\mathscr{M}}\delta^{*}_{\sharp}
and
{}^{\mathscr{M}}\eta^{*}_{\sharp}
given in (
5.3
) and (
5.4
), this follows directly from
2.6
(2) and
2.6
(1), which ensure that the diagram

\textstyle{{\mathbb{D}_{X}^{\mathscr{P}}f^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}[d]\mathbb{D}_{Y}^{\mathscr{P}}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{f}^{\mathscr{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{f}^{\mathscr{M}}}
\textstyle{{(\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{P}}_{X})^{2}f_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}(d)[d]\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\varepsilon^{\mathscr{P}}_{X})^{-1}}
\textstyle{{\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{P}}_{X}f_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}[d]\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\varepsilon_{f}^{\mathscr{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\nu_{Y}^{\mathscr{M}}}
\textstyle{{f^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}(d)[d](\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{P}}_{Y})^{2}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}}
\scriptstyle{(\varepsilon^{\mathscr{P}}_{Y})^{-1}}
\textstyle{f_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}(d)[d]\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}}
\textstyle{{f_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}(d)[d]\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{P}}_{Y}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\nu^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}}
\textstyle{{f_{\mathscr{P}}^{*}(d)[d]\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}(\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y})^{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\varepsilon^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y})^{-1}}

is commutative.

∎

The pair
(j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}},i^{*}_{\mathscr{M}})
is conservative, since so is the pair
(j^{*}_{\mathscr{P}},i^{*}_{\mathscr{P}})
. This follows from the existence of the isomorphisms
\theta^{\mathscr{M}}_{j}
,
\theta^{\mathscr{M}}_{i}
and the fact that
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}
is a conservative functor.

To finish the proof of
5.1
, it remains to check that, if
s
is the zero section of
p:\mathbf{A}^{1}_{X}\rightarrow X
, then
p^{\mathscr{M}}_{\sharp}s^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}
is an equivalence of categories. By construction

p^{\mathscr{M}}_{\sharp}s^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}=\mathbb{D}_{X}^{\mathscr{M}}p_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}(-1)[-2]\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{X}}s^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}.

Note that the isomorphism
\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{X}}s^{\mathscr{P}}_{*}\simeq s^{\mathscr{P}}_{*}\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{P}}_{X}
exists in the category of (constructible) étale motives. Therefore,
the compatibility of the Betti realization with the four operations (see
[
Ayo10
, Théorème 3.19]
) implies by the universal property of the categories of perverse motives that this isomorphism lifts to an isomorphism
\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{X}}s^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}\simeq s^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}
. As a consequence, we get an isomorphism

p^{\mathscr{M}}_{\sharp}s^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}\simeq\mathbb{D}_{X}^{\mathscr{M}}p_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}s^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}(-1)[-2]\mathbb{D}^{\mathscr{M}}_{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{X}}\simeq\mathrm{Id}(-1)[-2].

This shows that
p^{\mathscr{M}}_{\sharp}s^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}
is an equivalence of categories and concludes the proof of
5.1
.

5.3.
Complement to the main theorem

The following proposition complements
5.1
.

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a morphism of quasi-projective
k
-varieties. Then, the natural transformations

\xi^{\mathscr{M}}_{f}:\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}f^{!}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow f^{!}_{\mathscr{P}}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}\qquad\rho^{\mathscr{M}}_{f}:f^{\mathscr{P}}_{!}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}\rightarrow\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Y}f^{\mathscr{M}}_{!}

are invertible.

Proof.

By
[
Ayo10
, Théorème 3.4]
, it just remains to check that
\xi^{\mathscr{M}}_{i}
is invertible if
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
is a closed immersion. Let
j:U\hookrightarrow X
be the open immersion of the complement of
Z
in
X
. Then, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{{i^{\mathscr{P}}_{*}i^{!}_{\mathscr{P}}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{j^{\mathscr{P}}_{*}j^{*}_{\mathscr{P}}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\textstyle{{i^{\mathscr{P}}_{*}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Z}i^{!}_{\mathscr{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\xi^{\mathscr{M}}_{i}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{j^{\mathscr{P}}_{*}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{U}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\scriptstyle{(\theta_{j}^{\mathscr{M}})^{-1}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}i^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}i^{!}_{\mathscr{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma^{\mathscr{M}}_{i}}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}j^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{+1}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{j}^{\mathscr{M}}}

which implies that the image of
\xi^{\mathscr{M}}_{i}
under
i^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}
is invertible since all the other morphisms are. Therefore
\xi^{\mathscr{M}}_{i}
is also invertible.
∎

5.4.
Some consequences

In this subsection, we draw some immediate consequences of the main theorem (
5.1
).

Geometric local systems are motivic

A
\mathbf{Q}
-local system
\mathscr{L}
on a quasi-projective
k
-variety
X
will be called geometric if there exists a smooth proper morphism
g:Z\rightarrow X
such that
\mathscr{L}=R^{i}g_{*}\mathbf{Q}
for some integer
i\in\mathbf{Z}
. We will say that
\mathscr{L}
is motivic if there exists an object
L
in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X))
such that
\mathscr{L}
and
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}(L)
are isomorphic in the category
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{P}(X))
.

Corollary 5.5
.

A geometric
\mathbf{Q}
-local system
\mathscr{L}
on a quasi-projective
k
-variety
X
is motivic.

Proof.

If the local system
\mathscr{L}
is geometric, there exists a smooth proper morphism
g:Z\rightarrow X
such that
\mathscr{L}=R^{i}g_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{Z}
for some integer
i\in\mathbf{Z}
. Then
\mathscr{L}
is the image under the functor
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}
of the perverse motive
{}^{\mathrm{c}}\mathrm{H}^{i}(g^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}\mathbf{Q}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Z})
, where
{}^{\mathrm{c}}\mathrm{H}^{i}
is the cohomological functor associated with the constructible
t
-structure (see below).
∎

Remark 5.6
.

In this remark, we denote by
\mathrm{H}^{i}
the standard cohomology functors on the category
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X))
.
Let
\mathscr{L}
be a geometric
\mathbf{Q}
-local system on a smooth quasi-projective variety of (pure) dimension
d
and choose a smooth proper morphism
g:Z\rightarrow X
, an integer
j\in\mathbf{Z}
such that
\mathscr{L}=R^{j}g_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{Z}
.
As
Z
is smooth and
g
is proper and smooth, the constructible sheaves

R^{i}g_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{Z}
are all
\mathbf{Q}
-local systems on
X
. Hence the complexes

(R^{i}g_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{Z})[d]
are perverse sheaves and therefore

(R^{i}g_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{Z})[d]={}^{p}H^{d+i}g_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{Z}
for every
i\in\mathbf{Z}
. In particular,
\mathscr{L}[d]={}^{p}H^{j+d}g_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{Z}
and it follows that
\mathscr{L}[d]
is the image under
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}
of the perverse motivic sheaf
A:=\mathrm{H}^{j+d}(g_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}\mathbf{Q}^{\mathscr{M}}_{Z})
.

Intersection cohomology

The four operations formalism allows the definition of a motivic avatar of intersection complexes. In particular, intersection cohomology groups with coefficients in geometric systems are motivic. More precisely:

Corollary 5.7
.

Let
X
be an irreducible quasi-projective
k
-variety and
\mathscr{L}
be a
\mathbf{Q}
-local system on a smooth dense open subscheme of
X
. If
\mathscr{L}
is motivic (in particular if
\mathscr{L}
is geometric), then the intersection cohomology group
IH^{i}(X,\mathscr{L})
, for
i\in\mathbf{Z}
, is canonically the Betti realization of a Nori motive over
k
.

Proof.

Let
d
be the dimension of
X
and
\mathscr{L}
be a
\mathbf{Q}
-local system on a smooth dense open subscheme
U
of
X
. Since
\mathscr{L}
is motivic, there exists an object
L\in\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(U))
such that
\mathscr{L}
is isomorphic to
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{U}(L)
. Since
\mathscr{L}[d]
is a perverse sheaf on
U
and
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{U}
is conservative, the complex
L[d]
is a perverse motivic sheaf on
U
that is belongs to
\mathscr{M}(U)
. Then, with the notation of
6.19
, the intersection complex

\mathrm{IC}_{X}(\mathscr{L}):=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits({}^{p}H^{0}j^{\mathscr{P}}_{!}\mathscr{L}[d]\rightarrow{}^{p}H^{0}j_{*}^{\mathscr{P}}\mathscr{L}[d])

is canonically isomorphic to the image under
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}
of the perverse motivic sheaf
j^{\mathscr{M}}_{!*}L[d]:=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(\mathrm{H}^{0}(j^{\mathscr{M}}_{!}L[d])\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(j^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}L[d]))
. This implies that

IH^{i}(X,\mathscr{L}):={\mathbf{H}}^{i-d}(X,\mathrm{IC}_{X}(\mathscr{L}))
is the Betti realization of the Nori motive
\mathrm{H}^{i-d}(\pi^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}j^{\mathscr{M}}_{!*}L[d])
where
\pi:X\rightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}k
is the structural morphism.

∎

This shows, in particular, that intersection cohomology groups carry a natural Hodge structure. If
X
is a smooth projective curve, and
\mathscr{L}
underlies a polarizable variation of Hodge structure, then the Hodge structure on the intersection cohomology groups was constructed by Zucker in
[
Zuc79
, (7.2) Theorem, (11.6) Theorem]
. In general, it follows from Saito’s work on mixed Hodge modules
[
Sai90b
]
and a different proof has been given by de Cataldo in
[
dC12
]
. We consider the weights in the next section (see
6.28
and
6.29
).

Leray spectral sequences

Let
f:X\rightarrow Y
be a morphism of quasi-projective
k
-varieties and
\mathscr{L}
be a
\mathbf{Q}
-local system on
X
. Then, we can associate with it two Leray spectral sequences in Betti cohomology: the classical one

{\mathbf{H}}^{r}(Y,R^{s}f_{*}\mathscr{L})\Longrightarrow H^{r+s}(X,\mathscr{L})

and the perverse one

{\mathbf{H}}^{r}(Y,{}^{p}H^{s}f_{*}\mathscr{L})\Longrightarrow H^{r+s}(X,\mathscr{L}).

The main theorem of Arapura’s
[
Ara05
]
shows that, if
\mathscr{L}=\mathbf{Q}_{X}
is the constant local system on
X
and the morphism
f
is projective, then the classical Leray sequence is motivic, that is, it is the realization of a spectral sequence in the abelian category of Nori motives over
k
(see precisely
[
Ara05
, Theorem 3.1]
).

This property is still true without the projectivity assumption and also more generally if the local system
\mathscr{L}
is geometric:

Corollary 5.8
.

If the local system
\mathscr{L}
is motivic (in particular if it is geometric), then the classical Leray spectral sequence and the perverse Leray spectral sequence are spectral sequences of Nori motives over
k
.

Proof.

The result follows from the functoriality of the direct image functors.
∎

In particular, the Leray spectral sequences are spectral sequences of (polarizable) mixed Hodge structures. The compatibility of the classical Leray spectral sequence result in Hodge theory was already proved by Zucker in
[
Zuc79
]
when
X
is a curve and more generally, for both spectral sequences, by Saito if
\mathscr{L}
underlies an admissible variation of mixed Hodge structures (see
[
Sai90b
]
). This result has been recovered by de Cataldo and Migliorini with different techniques in
[
dCM10
]
.

Nearby cycles

The theory developed here also shows that nearby cycles functors applied to perverse motives produce Nori motives.

Corollary 5.9
.

Let
X
be a quasi-projective
k
-variety,
f:X\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}_{k}
a flat morphism with smooth generic fiber
X_{\eta}
and
\mathscr{L}
be a
\mathbf{Q}
-local system on
X_{\eta}
. If

\mathscr{L}
is motivic (in particular if it is geometric), then, for every point
x\in X_{\sigma}(k)
and every integer
i\in\mathbf{Z}
, the Betti cohomology
H^{i}(\Psi_{f}(\mathscr{L})_{x})
of the nearby fiber is canonically a Nori motive over
k
.

Proof.

The nearby cycles functor
\psi_{f}:=\Psi_{f}[-1]
is
t
-exact for the perverse
t
-structure. Since it exists in the triangulated category of constructible étale motives (see
[
Ayo07b
]
) and the Betti realization is compatible with the nearby cycles functor by
[
Ayo10
, Proposition 4.9]
, the universal property ensures the existence of an exact functor
\psi_{f}^{\mathscr{M}}:\mathscr{M}(X_{\eta})\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(X_{\sigma})
and an invertible natural transformation
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X_{\sigma}}\psi_{f}^{\mathscr{M}}\simeq\psi_{f}\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X_{\eta}}
.

Let
d
be the dimension of the generic fiber
X_{\eta}
. Since
\mathscr{L}
is motivic, there exists an object
L
in
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X_{\eta}))
such that
\mathscr{L}
and
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X_{\eta}}(L)
are isomorphic. As
\mathscr{L}[d]
is perverse and
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X_{\eta}}
is conservative, the complex
L[d]
belongs to
\mathscr{M}(X_{\eta})
. So we conclude that
H^{i}(\Psi_{f}(\mathscr{L})_{x})
is the Betti realization of the Nori motive
\mathrm{H}^{i+1-d}(x^{*}\psi_{f}^{\mathscr{M}}L[d])
.

∎

Exponential motives

The perverse motives introduced in the present paper and their stability under the four operations could be used also in the study of exponential motives as introduced in Fresán—Jossen’s book
[
FJ18
]
. Indeed, recall that Kontsevich and Soibelman define an exponential mixed Hodge structure as a mixed Hodge module
A
on the complex affine line
\mathbf{A}^{1}_{\mathbf{C}}
such that
p_{*}A=0
, where
p:\mathbf{A}^{1}_{\mathbf{C}}\rightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(\mathbf{C})
is the projection (see the paper
[
KS11
]
of Kontsevich and Soibelman). Their definition can be mimicked in the motivic context and the abelian category of exponential Nori motives can be defined as the full subcategory of
\mathscr{M}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{k})
formed by the objects which have no global cohomology.

Constructible
t
-structure

Let us conclude by a possible comparison with Arapura’s construction from
[
Ara13
]
. Let
X
be a
k
-variety and consider the following full subcategories of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X))

\displaystyle{}^{c}\mathrm{D}^{\leqslant 0}

\displaystyle:=\{A\in\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X)):H^{k}(x^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}A)=0,\;\forall\;x\in X,\forall\;k>0\},

\displaystyle{}^{c}\mathrm{D}^{\geqslant 0}

\displaystyle:=\{A\in\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X)):H^{k}(x^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}A)=0,\;\forall\;x\in X,\forall\;k<0\}.

As in 4.6. Remarks of M. Saito’s
[
Sai90b
]
(see also
[
Ara13
, Theorem C.0.12]
), we can check that these categories define a
t
-structure on
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(X))
.

Let
{}^{\mathsf{ct}}\kern-3.00003pt\mathscr{M}(X)
be the heart of this
t
-structure. Then, the functor
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{X}
induces a faithful exact functor from
{}^{\mathsf{ct}}\kern-3.00003pt\mathscr{M}(X)
into the abelian category of constructible sheaves of
\mathbf{Q}
-vector spaces on
X
. Then, using the universal property of the category of constructible motives
\mathcal{M}(X,\mathbf{Q})
constructed by Arapura in
[
Ara13
]
, we get a faithful exact functor
\mathcal{M}(X,\mathbf{Q})\rightarrow{}^{\mathsf{ct}}\kern-3.00003pt\mathscr{M}(X)
. Is this functor an equivalence? If
X=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}k
, then
both categories are equivalent to the abelian category of Nori motives, so this functor is an equivalence.

6.
Weights

In this section, we will use results on motives and weight structures from Bondarko’s and Hébert’s papers
[
Bon14
,
H1́1
]
.
To apply these references directly in our context, we will make use of the fact that, if

S
is a Noetherian scheme of finite dimension, then Ayoub’s
category
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)
is canonically equivalent to the
category of constructible Beĭlinson motives studied in Cisinski and
Déglise’s book
[
CD19
]
.
This follows from
[
CD19
, Theorem 16.2.18]
and will
henceforth be used without further comment. (Note also that, though the authors of
[
Bon14
,
H1́1
]
have chosen to use Beĭlinson’s motives, étale motives could have been used.)

6.1.
Continuity of the abelian hull

Remember that, in chapter 5 of Neeman’s book
[
Nee01
]
, there are
four constructions of the abelian hull of a triangulated category.
The first one gives a lax
2
-functor from the
2
-category of triangulated
categories to that of abelian categories, but the other three constructions
give strict
2
-functors. If we use the fourth
construction, which Neeman calls
D(\matheusm S)
(see
[
Nee01
, Definition 5.2.1]
), then the following proposition is immediate.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
\matheusm S
be a triangulated category, and
suppose that we have an equivalence of triangulated categories

\matheusm S\xrightarrow{\sim}2-\varinjlim_{i\in I}\matheusm{S}_{i}
, where
I
is a small filtered category.

Then the canonical functor
\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\matheusm{S})\rightarrow 2-\varinjlim_{i\in I}\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\matheusm{S}_{i})
is an equivalence of abelian categories.

6.2.
Étale realization and
\ell
-adic
perverse Nori motives

Let
S
be a Noetherian excellent scheme finite-dimensional scheme,
let
\ell
be a prime
number invertible over
S
; we assume
that
S
is a
\mathbf{Q}
-scheme.
(By Exposé XVIII-A of
[
ILO14
]
, the hypotheses above imply
Hypothesis 5.1 in Ayoub’s paper
[
Ayo14a
]
.)
Under this hypothesis,
Ayoub has constructed an étale
\ell
-adic realization functor
on
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)
, compatible with pullbacks.

Theorem 6.2
.

(See
[
Ayo14a
]
sections 5 and 10.)
Denote by
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{c}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
the category of constructible

\ell
-adic complexes on
S
.
Then we have a triangulated functor
\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}_{S}:\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{c}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
for every
S

and, for every morphism
f:S\rightarrow S^{\prime}
, with
S^{\prime}
satisfying the same
hypotheses as
S
,
we have an
invertible natural transformation

\theta_{f}:f^{*}\circ\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}_{Y}\rightarrow\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}_{X}\circ f^{*}.

Using results of Gabber (see
[
Gab04
]
, and also sections 4 and 5
of Fargues’s article
[
Far09
]
), we can construct an abelian category

\mathscr{P}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
of
\ell
-adic
perverse sheaves on
S
, satisfying
all the usual properties. In particular, we get a perverse cohomology
functor
{}^{p}H^{0}_{\ell}:={}^{p}H^{0}\circ\mathfrak{R}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}_{S}:\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
.

Definition 6.3
.

Let
S
be as above.
The Abelian category of
\ell
-adic perverse motives on
S

is the Abelian category

\mathscr{M}(S)_{\ell}:=\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S),{}^{p}H^{0}_{\ell}).

By construction, the functor
{}^{p}H^{0}_{\ell}
has a factorization

\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)\xrightarrow{{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}}\mathscr{M}(S)_{\ell}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{S,\ell}}\mathscr{P}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})

where
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{S,\ell}
is a faithful exact functor
and
{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}
is a homological functor.

By the universal property of
\mathscr{M}(S)_{\ell}
, we also get pullback
functors between these categories as soon as the pullback functor
between the categories of
\ell
-adic complexes preserves the category
of perverse sheaves.

We will use the following important fact:
If we fix a base field
k
of characteristic
0

and only consider schemes
that are quasi-projective over
k
, then the main theorem (stated in

5.1
) stays true for the
categories
\mathscr{M}(S)_{\ell}
.
Of course,
we have to replace
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)
and the Betti realization functor by

\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{c}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
and the étale realization functor
in all the statements. Indeed, the proof of the main theorem, of of the statements that
it uses, still work if we use the
\ell
-adic étale realization instead of the Betti realization.
The only result that requires a slightly different proof is
3.13
: we have to
show that the
\ell
-adic realization of Ayoub’s logarithmic motive
{\mathscr{L}}og^{\vee}_{n}
is the local system
used in Beilison’s construction of the unipotent nearby cycle functor (see 1.1 and 1.2 of
Beilinson’s
[
Bei87b
]
or Definition 5.2.1 of
[
Mor18
]
). As in the proof of

3.13
, it suffices to check this for
n=1
, and then it follows from
Lemma 11.22 of Ayoub’s
[
Ayo14a
]
.

6.3.
Mixed horizontal perverse sheaves

Let
k
be a field and
S
be a
k
-scheme of finite type.
Suppose that
k
is finitely generated over its prime field.
We also fix
a prime number
\ell
invertible over
S
.
The category

\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{m}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
of mixed horizontal

\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}
-complexes
and its perverse t-structure
with heart
\mathscr{P}_{m}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
(the category of mixed horizontal

\ell
-adic perverse sheaves on
S
)
were constructed
in Huber’s article
[
Hub97
]
(see also
[
Mor18
, section 2]
). We recall the definition quickly and refer to
[
Hub97
]
and

[
Mor18
]
for the details. First we consider the category

\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{h}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
of horizontal complexes on
S
, which is
by definition the
2
-colimit of the categories
