\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{c}}(\mathscr{X},\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
,
where
\mathscr{X}
runs over all flat finite type models of
X
over
regular subalgebras
A
of
k
that are of finite type over
\mathbf{Z}
and
have
k
as their fraction field. There is an obvious functor

\eta^{*}:\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{h}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{c}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
, which is triangulated and
conservative, and a perverse
t
-structure on
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{h}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})

that is characterized by the fact that
\eta^{*}
is
t
-exact. Also,
the functor
\eta^{*}
is fully faithful on the heart of this
t
-structure
(
[
Mor18
, Proposition 2.6.2]
).

We say that an object of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{h}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
if it extends to a complex

K
on a model
\mathscr{X}
of
X
as before such that all the
(ordinary) cohomology sheaves of
K
are successive extensions of
punctually pure sheaves in the sense of Deligne’s
[
Del80
]
.
The category
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{m}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
of mixed horizontal complexes is the
full subcategory of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{h}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
whose objects are mixed complexes.
The perverse
t
-structure on
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{h}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
restricts to a

t
-structure on
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{m}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
, whose heart is the category

{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathrm{m}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
of mixed horizontal perverse sheaves; this last
category is a full subcategory of the heart of the perverse
t
-structure
on
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{h}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
, so
\eta^{*}
induces a fully faithful functor

{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathrm{m}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
.

Now we want to show that the realization functor
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{S,\ell}:\mathscr{M}(S)_{\ell}\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
factors
through the fully faithful functor
\mathscr{P}_{m}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
.

We have a continuity theorem for the categories of étale motives, proved by Ayoub
in
[
Ayo15
, Corollaire 1.A.3]
and
[
Ayo14a
, Corollaire 3.22]
and by Cisinski–Déglise in
[
CD19
, Proposition 15.1.6]
).

Theorem 6.4
.

Let
S
be a Noetherian scheme of finite dimension.
Suppose that we have
S=\varprojlim_{i\in I}S_{i}
, where all the
S_{i}

are finite-dimensional Noetherian schemes and all the transition maps

S_{i}\rightarrow S_{j}
are affine. Then the canonical functor

2-\varinjlim_{i\in I}\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S_{i})\rightarrow\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)
is an equivalence of
monoidal triangulated categories.

Using the definition of mixed horizontal
\ell
-adic complexes, we immediately
get the following corollary.

Corollary 6.5
.

Let
S
and
\ell
be as in the beginning of this subsection.
Then the étale realization functor
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{c}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
factors
through a functor
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{h}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
.

Corollary 6.6
.

With the notation of the previous corollary, the essential image of the functor

\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)\rightarrow\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{h}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
is contained in the full subcategory

\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{m}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
.
In particular, the perverse cohomology functor

{}^{p}H^{0}_{\ell}:\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
factors through the subcategory
\mathscr{P}_{m}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
.

Proof.

This follows from the facts that
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)
is generated by the Tate
twists of motives of smooth
S
-schemes (see Definition 15.1.1 and
Proposition 15.1.4 of
[
CD19
]
) and that mixed horizontal complexes
are preserved by direct images and Tate twists (see

[
Hub97
, Proposition 3.2]
for direct images, the stability
by Tate twists is easy).

∎

Corollary 6.7
.

The essential image of the
realization functor
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{S,\ell}:\mathscr{M}(S)_{\ell}\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
is contained in the
subcategory
\mathscr{P}_{m}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
.

We will also denote the resulting faithful exact functor
\mathscr{M}(S)\rightarrow\mathscr{P}_{m}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
by

\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{S,\ell}
.

Remark 6.8
.

Suppose that
k
is not necessarily finitely generated
over its prime field. We define
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{m}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
as the
2
-colimit
of the categories
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{m}}(S^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
, for
S^{\prime}
a model
of
S
over a finitely generated subfield of
k
. This category inherits
a perverse
t
-structure from the perverse
t
-structures on the

\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{m}}(S^{\prime},\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
, whose heart we denote by
{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathrm{m}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
.
The obvious functor
{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathrm{m}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
is only
exact faithful in general (not necessarily fully faithful), but the
the perverse cohomology functor

{}^{p}H^{0}_{\ell}:\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})

still factors
through this functor as in Corollary
6.6

(by Theorem
6.4
), so we get a faithful exact
realization functor
\mathscr{M}(S)_{\ell}\rightarrow{\mathscr{P}}_{\mathrm{m}}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
.

6.4.
Continuity for perverse Nori motives

Like the triangulated category of motives, the category of
perverse Nori motives satisfies a continuity property.

Proposition 6.9
.

Let
S
be a scheme and
\ell
be a prime number
satisfying the conditions of

6.2
. We assume that

S=\varprojlim_{i\in I}S_{i}
, where

(S_{i})_{i\in I}
is a directed projective system of schemes
satisfying the same conditions as
S
, and in which the transition maps
are affine. We also assume that the pullback by any transition map

S_{i}\rightarrow S_{j}
preserves the category of perverse sheaves,
and that there exists
a\in\mathbf{Z}
such that, if

f_{i}:S\rightarrow S_{i}
is the canonical map, then
f^{*}_{i}[a]

preserves the category of perverse sheaves for every
i\in I
.
Under these hypotheses, the functors
f_{i}^{*}[a]
induce a functor

2-\varinjlim_{i\in I}\mathscr{M}(S_{i})_{\ell}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(S)_{\ell},

and this functor is full and essentially surjective.

If moreover
the canonical exact functor
2-\varinjlim_{i\in I}\mathscr{P}(S_{i},\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})

induced by the
f_{i}^{*}[a]
is faithful,
then the canonical functor

2-\varinjlim_{i\in I}\mathscr{M}(S_{i})_{\ell}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(S)_{\ell}

is an equivalence of abelian categories.

Proof.

This follows from
6.1
and
6.4
.

∎

Corollary 6.10
.

Let
S
and
\ell

be as above, and suppose also that
S
is integral.
Then, if

\eta
is the generic point of
S
, the canonical
exact functor

2-\varinjlim_{U}\mathscr{M}(U)_{\ell}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(\eta)_{\ell},

where the colimit is taken over all nonempty affine open subschemes of
S

and where the image of
K_{U}\in\Ob\mathscr{M}(U)_{\ell}
is
K_{U,\eta}[-\dim S]
,
is an equivalence of categories.

Proof.

By
6.9
, it suffices to check
that the similar functor

2-\varinjlim_{U}\mathscr{P}(U,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(\eta,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})

is faithful. Let
K
be an object of

2-\varinjlim_{U}\mathscr{P}(U,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
whose image in
\mathscr{P}(\eta,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
is

0
, and let
U
be a nonempty open affine subscheme of
S
such that

K
comes from an object
K^{\prime}
of
\mathscr{P}(U,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
. After shrinking

U
(which does not change
K
), we may assume that

K^{\prime}[-\dim S]
is a local system. Then the condition
K^{\prime}_{\eta}[-\dim S]=0

implies that this local system is zero, hence that
K=0
.

∎

6.5.
Comparison of the different categories
of perverse Nori motives

In the next proposition, we compare the
\ell
-adic definition of perverse motives with the one used previously and obtained via the Betti realization.

Proposition 6.11
.

Suppose that
k
is a field
of characteristic
0
and that
S
is quasi-projective over
k
.

We write

\rho_{\ell}
for the canonical exact functor

\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S))\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
induced by
{}^{p}H^{0}_{\ell}
.
If

\sigma
is an embedding of
k
into
\mathbf{C}
, then
we also have an exact functor

\rho_{\sigma}
from
\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S))
to
\mathscr{P}(S)
induced by
{}^{p}H^{0}
.
Then:

(1)

If
\ell,\ell^{\prime}
are two
prime numbers,
then
\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits\rho_{\ell}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits\rho_{\ell^{\prime}}
.
In particular, we get
a canonical equivalence of abelian categories
\mathscr{M}(S)_{\ell}=\mathscr{M}(S)_{\ell^{\prime}}
.

(2)

If
\sigma:k\rightarrow\mathbf{C}
is
an embedding, then
\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits\rho_{\ell}=\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits\rho_{\sigma}
.
In particular, we get
a canonical equivalence of abelian categories
\mathscr{M}(S)_{\ell}=\mathscr{M}(S)
.

Proof.

We first treat the case
S=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}k
.
If
k
can be embedded in
\mathbf{C}
, then (2) follows from Huber’s
construction of mixed realizations in Huber’s
[
Hub00
]
, and (1) follows
from (2). In the general case,
(1) follows from the case where

k
can be embedded in
\mathbf{C}
and from
6.9
,
applied to the family of
subfields of
k
that can be embedded in
\mathbf{C}
.

Now we treat the case of a general
k
-scheme
S
.
As in the first case, (1) follows from (2)
and from
6.9
. So suppose that we have
an embedding
\sigma:k\rightarrow\mathbf{C}
.
We prove the desired result by induction on the dimension of
S
.
The case
\dim S=0
has already been treated, so we may assume that
\dim S>0

and that the result is known for all the schemes of lower dimension.
We denote by
M\mapsto[M]
the canonical functor
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)\rightarrow\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S))
;
as
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)
is a triangulated category, this is a fully faithful
functor. Let
X
be an object of
\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S))
.
By construction of
\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S))
,
there
exists a morphism
N\rightarrow M
in
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)
such that
X
is the
cokernel of
[N]\rightarrow[M]
. Then
\rho_{\ell}(X)
is the cokernel
of
{}^{p}H^{0}_{\ell}(N)\rightarrow{}^{p}H^{0}_{\ell}(M)
, so
\rho_{\ell}(X)=0
if and only

{}^{p}H^{0}_{\ell}(N)\rightarrow{}^{p}H^{0}_{\ell}(M)
is surjective. Similarly,
\rho_{\sigma}(X)=0

if and only if
{}^{p}H^{0}(N)\rightarrow{}^{p}H^{0}(M)
is surjective. We can check these
conditions on a Zariski open covering of
S
, so we may assume that

S
is affine.
Choose a nonempty
smooth open subset
U
of
S
such that the restrictions to
U
of

\rho_{\ell}(M)
,
\rho_{\ell}(N)
,

\rho_{\sigma}(M)
and

\rho_{\sigma}(N)
are all locally constant sheaves placed in degree

-\dim S
.
As
S
is affine,
after shrinking
U
, we may assume that
U
is the complement
of the vanishing set of a nonzero function
f\in\mathcal{O}(S)
.
By Proposition 3.1 of Beilinson’s
[
Bei87b
]
, we have
that
\rho_{\ell}(N)\rightarrow\rho_{\ell}(M)
is surjective
if and only if both

\rho_{\ell}(N)_{\mid U}\rightarrow\rho_{\ell}(M)_{\mid U}

and
{}^{p}\Phi_{f}^{\mathscr{M}}\rho_{\ell}(N)\rightarrow{}^{p}\Phi_{f}^{\mathscr{M}}\rho_{\ell}(M)
are,
which is equivalent to the surjectivity of

\rho_{\ell}(N_{\mid U})\rightarrow\rho_{\ell}(M_{\mid U})

and
\rho_{\ell}(\Phi_{f}N)\rightarrow\rho_{\ell}(\Phi_{f}M)
. We have a similar statement
for
\rho_{\sigma}
.
As
\dim(S-U)<\dim(S)
, we can use the induction hypothesis
to reduce to the case
S=U
.
It suffices to check the result on an étale cover of
S
, so we
may assume that
S
has a rational point
x
.
Let
i:x\rightarrow S
be the obvious inclusion.
As
\rho_{\ell}(N)[-\dim S]
and

\rho_{\ell}(M)[-\dim S]
are locally constant sheaves on
S
, the morphism

\rho_{\ell}(N)\rightarrow\rho_{\ell}(M)
is surjective if and only if

\rho_{\ell}(i^{*}N[-\dim S])\rightarrow\rho_{\ell}(i^{*}M[-\dim S])
is, and similarly
for
\rho_{\sigma}
. So we are reduced to the result on
the scheme
x
, which we have
already treated.
∎

Corollary 6.12
.

Let
k
be a field of characteristic
0
and
S
a quasi-projective
scheme over
k
. We have a canonical
\mathbf{Q}
-linear
abelian category of perverse Nori motives

\mathscr{M}(S)
, together with a cohomological functor
{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}:\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(S)
, with a
\ell
-adic realization functor

\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{S,\ell}:\mathscr{M}(S)\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
for every prime number

\ell
, with a Betti realization functor

\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{S,\sigma}:\mathscr{M}(S)\rightarrow\mathscr{P}(S)
for every embedding

\sigma:k\rightarrow\mathbf{C}
, and it has a formalism of the 4 operations, duality,
unipotent nearby and vanishing cycles compatible with all these operations.

We fix a field
k
of characteristic zero and a quasi-projective
scheme
S
over
k
. We first define weights via the
\ell
-adic realizations.

Definition 6.13
.

Let
w\in\mathbf{Z}
.
Let
K
be an object of
\mathscr{M}(S)
. We say that
K
is of weight
\leq w

(resp.
\geq w
) if
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{S,\ell}(K)\in\Ob(\mathscr{P}_{m}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell}))

is of weight
\leq w
(resp.

\geq w
) for every
prime number
\ell
. We say that
K
is pure of
weight
w
if it is both of weight
\leq w
and of weight
\geq w
.

In
6.18
, we will a more intrinsic definition of weights that does not use
the realization functors.

Definition 6.14
.

A
weight filtration
on an object
K
of
\mathscr{M}(S)
is
an increasing filtration
W_{\bullet}K
on
K
such that
W_{i}K=0
for

i
small enough,
W_{i}K=K
for
i
big enough, and
W_{i}K/W_{i-1}K
is
pure of weight
i
for every
i\in\mathbf{Z}
.

The next result follows immediately from the similar result in
the categories of mixed horizontal perverse sheaves (see Proposition 3.4 and Lemma 3.8 of
Huber’s
[
Hub97
]
).

Proposition 6.15
.

Let
K,L
be objects of
\mathscr{M}(S)
, and let
w\in\mathbf{Z}
.

(1)

If
K
is of weight
\leq w
(resp.
\geq w
), so is every
subquotient of
K
.

(2)

If
K
is of weight
\leq w
and
L
is of weight
\geq w+1
,
then
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{M}(S)}(K,L)=0
.

Recall that, if
A
and
B
are objects of an abelian category
endowed with increasing filtrations
(F_{i}A)_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}
and

(F_{i}B)_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}
, then a morphism
u:A\rightarrow B
is called

compatible
(resp.
strictly compatible
)
with the filtrations if, for every
i\in\mathbf{Z}
, we have

u(F_{i}A)\subset F_{i}B
(resp.
u(F_{i}A)=u(A)\cap F_{i}B
).

Corollary 6.16
.

A weight filtration on an object of
\mathscr{M}(S)
is unique
if it exists, and morphisms of
\mathscr{M}(S)
are strictly compatible with
weights filtrations.
In particular, if an object of
\mathscr{M}(S)
has a weight filtration, then so do
all its subquotients.

6.6.
Application of Bondarko’s weight structures

Let
S
be as in the previous subsection. We will now make use of Bondarko’s
Chow weight structure on
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)
.
Let
\Chow(S)
be the full subcategory of
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)
whose objects
are direct factors of finite direct sums of objects of the form

f_{!}\mathbf{Q}_{X}(d)[2d]
, with
f:X\rightarrow S
a proper morphism from a smooth
k
-scheme

X
to
S
and
d\in\mathbf{Z}
. Then, as shown by Hébert in
[
H1́1
, Theorem 3.3]
, and also by Bondarko in
[
Bon14
, Theorem 2.1]
, there exists a unique weight structure
on
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)
with heart
\Chow(S)

(see
[
H1́1
, Definition 1.5]
or
[
Bon14
, Definition 1.5]
for the definition of a weight structure).

In particular, for every object
K
of
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)
, there exists an
exact triangle
A\rightarrow K\rightarrow B\xrightarrow{+1}
(not unique) such that

A
(resp.
B
) is a direct factor of a successive extension of objects of

\Chow(S)[i]
with
i\leq 0
(resp.
i\geq 1
).

Proposition 6.17
.

Every object of
\mathscr{M}(S)
has a weight filtration. Moreover,
if
S=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}k
and
\sigma
is an embedding of
k
in
\mathbf{C}
, then the
notion of weights of
6.13

coincides with that of Section 10.2.2 of the book
[
HMS17
]
of Huber and Müller-Stach.

Proof.

We first prove that, if
M
is an object of
\Chow(S)
,
then
{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(M)
is pure of weight
0
in our sense, and also in the sense
of
[
HMS17
, Section 10.2.2]
if
S=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}k
with
k
embeddable in

\mathbf{C}
. The second statement is actually an immediate consequence of

[
HMS17
, Definition 10.2.4]
and of the motivic Chow’s lemma
(see for example
[
H1́1
, Lemma 3.1]
). To prove the first statement,
by definition of the weights on
\mathscr{P}_{m}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})
,
we may assume that
k
is finitely generated
over
\mathbf{Q}
; then the statement follows immediately from
[
BBD82
, 5.1.14]

(see the remark on page 116 of
[
Hub97
]
).

Then we note that every object of
\mathscr{M}(S)
is a quotient of an
object of the form
{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(M)
, for
M\in\Ob(\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S))
(because
this is true for objects of
\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S))
). So it suffices
to prove the result for objects in the essential image of

{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}
. Let
M\in\Ob(\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S))
, and let
K={}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(M)
.
Let
w\in\mathbf{Z}
.
By the first part of the proof,
if
M

is a direct factor of a successive extension of objects
of
\Chow(S)[i]
with
i\leq w
(resp
i\geq w+1
), then
{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(M)

is of weight
\leq w
(resp.
\geq w+1
) in our sense, and also in the
sense of
[
HMS17
]
when this applies. In general, using the Chow weight structure of Bondarko, we can find an exact triangle

A\rightarrow M\rightarrow B\xrightarrow{+1}
such that
A
(resp.
B
)
is a direct factor of a successive extension of objects
of
\Chow(S)[i]
with
i\leq w
(resp
i\geq w+1
). Applying
{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}
,
we get an exact sequence
{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(A)\rightarrow K\rightarrow{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(B)
,
with
{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(A)
of weight
\leq w
and
{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(B)
of weight
\geq w+1
.
If we set
W_{w}K=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits({}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(A)\rightarrow K)
, then
W_{w}K
is of weight
\leq w

and
K/W_{w}K
is of weight
\geq w+1
. This defines a weight filtration
on
K
.
∎

Weights and the related weight filtration so far have been defined and constructed for perverse motives via
the
\ell
-adic realizations. As we shall see now, we can also define weights more directly.
Let
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)_{w\leq i}
be the full subcategory of
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)
whose objects are direct factors of successive extensions of objects of

\Chow(S)[w]
with
w\leq i
and consider the Abelian category

\mathscr{M}(S)_{w\leq i}:=\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)_{w\leq i},{}^{p}H^{0}_{\ell}),

for some
prime number
\ell
.
It follows from
6.11
that this category, up to an equivalence, does not depend on
\ell
. Indeed, the universal property provides a commutative diagram (up to isomorphisms of functors)

\textstyle{{\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)_{w\leq i}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{I}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)_{w\leq i})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{J}
\scriptstyle{\varrho_{\ell}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{}^{p}H^{0}_{\ell}}
\textstyle{{\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S))}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\rho_{\ell}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{P}_{m}(S,\mathbf{Q}_{\ell})}

where
I
is the inclusion and
J,\varrho_{\ell}
are exact functors. As by construction
\mathscr{M}(S)_{w\leq i}:=\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)_{w\leq i})/\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits\varrho_{\ell}
it suffices to show that
\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits\varrho_{\ell}
is independent on
\ell
. Let
A
be an object in
\mathbf{A}^{\mathrm{ad}}(\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)_{w\leq i})
. Since
A
belongs to
\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits\varrho_{\ell}
if and only if
J(A)
belongs to
\mathop{\mathrm{Ker}}\nolimits\rho_{\ell}
our claim follows from
6.11
.

The inclusion
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)_{w\leq i}\subseteq\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)
induces a faithful exact functor

u_{i}:\mathscr{M}(S)_{w\leq i}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(S).

Let
K
be an object in
\mathscr{M}(S)
. Given an object
(L,\alpha:u_{i}(L)\rightarrow K)
in the slice category
\mathscr{M}(S)_{w\leq i}/K
we can consider the subobject
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits\alpha
of
K
and define
\matheusm W_{i}K
to be the union of all such subobjects in
K
, that is, we set

\matheusm W_{i}K:=\mathop{\mathrm{colim}}_{(L,\alpha)\in\mathscr{M}(S)_{w\leq i}/K}\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits\alpha.

This construction is functorial in
K
(and moreover using the inclusion of
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)_{w\leq i}
in
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)_{w\leq i+1}
it is easy to see that it defines a filtration on
K
).

Proposition 6.18
.

Let
K\in\mathscr{M}(S)
. Then,
W_{i}K=\matheusm W_{i}K
, for every integer
i\in\mathbf{Z}
.

Proof.

As observed in the proof of
6.17
, if
M
belongs to
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)_{w\leq i}
, then
{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(M)
is of weight
\leq i
. Hence, the functor
u_{i}
takes its values in the Abelian subcategory of
\mathscr{M}(S)
formed by the objects of weight
\leq i
. As a consequence, for
(L,\alpha)
in the slice category
\mathscr{M}(S)_{w\leq i}/K
, the subobject
\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits\alpha
of
K
is of weight
\leq i
and therefore
\matheusm W_{i}K\subseteq W_{i}K
.

Conversely, there exists an epimorphism
e:{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(M)\twoheadrightarrow K
where
M
belongs to
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)
. By construction

W_{i}{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(M):=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits\big({}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(A)\rightarrow{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(M)\big)\subseteq\matheusm W_{i}{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(M)

where
A
is an object of
\mathbf{DA}_{\mathsf{ct}}(S)_{w\leq i}
that fits in an exact triangle
A\rightarrow M\rightarrow B\xrightarrow{+1}
such that
B

is a direct factor of a successive extension of objects
of
\Chow(S)[w]
with
w\geq i+1
.
Therefore, since the weight filtration on
K
is the induced filtration (see
6.16
), we get

W_{i}K=e(W_{i}{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(M))\subseteq e(\matheusm W_{i}{}^{p}H^{0}_{\mathscr{M}}(M))\subseteq\matheusm W_{i}K.

This concludes the proof.
∎

6.7.
The intermediate extension functor

Recall the definition of the intermediate extension functor, that already appeared in the proof of
5.7
.

Definition 6.19
.

Let
j:S\rightarrow T
be a quasi-finite morphism between
quasi-projective
k
-schemes. We define a functor

j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}:\mathscr{M}(S)\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(T)

by

j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}(K)=\mathop{\mathrm{Im}}\nolimits(\mathrm{H}^{0}(j_{!}^{\mathscr{M}}K)\rightarrow\mathrm{H}^{0}(j_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}K)).

Note that, as
j
is quasi-finite, the functor
j_{!}^{\mathscr{M}}
is right exact
and the functor
j_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}
is left exact. In particular, the functor

j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}
preserves injective and surjective morphisms, but it is not
exact in general.

Proposition 6.20
.

Let
j:S\rightarrow T
be an open immersion, and let

w\in\mathbf{Z}
.
Then, if
K\in\Ob\mathscr{M}(S)
is of weight
\leq w
(resp. of weight
\geq w
, resp.
pure of weight
w
), so is
j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}K
.

Also, the functor
j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}
is exact on the full abelian subcategory
of objects that are pure of weight
w
.

Proof.

It suffices to show these statement for mixed
\ell
-adic
perverse sheaves. The first statement follows from
[
BBD82
, 5.3.2]

(more precisely, if
j
is not affine,
it follows from
[
BBD82
, 5.1.14 and
5.3.1]
). The second statement follows from
[
Mor18
, Corollary 9.4]
.
∎

6.8.
Pure objects

Let us start with the definition of objects with strict support on a given closed subscheme.

Definition 6.21
.

Let
Z
be a closed integral subscheme of
S
, and denote
the immersion
Z\rightarrow S
by
i
.
We say that an object
K
of
\mathscr{M}(S)
has strict support
Z
if

K_{\mid S-Z}=0
and if, for every nonempty open subset
j:U\rightarrow Z
, the adjunction
morphism
K\rightarrow(ij)_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}(ij)_{\mathscr{M}}^{*}K
is injective and induces an
isomorphism between
K
and

(ij)_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}(ij)_{\mathscr{M}}^{*}K
.

Remark 6.22
.

For example, if
K_{\mid S-Z}=0
and
if there exists a smooth dense open subset
j:U\rightarrow Z
such that

\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{U}(K_{\mid U})[-\dim U]
(or any
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{U,\ell}(K_{\mid U})[-\dim U]
for
some prime number
\ell
) is locally constant and
K_{Z}=j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}(K_{\mid U})
,
then
K
has strict support
Z
.
Indeed, this follows from the similar result for perverse sheaves, which
follows from
[
BBD82
, 4.3.2]
(note that the proof of this result does
not use the hypothesis that
\mathrm{L}
is irreducible).

Proposition 6.23
.

(Compare with
[
BBD82
, 5.3.8]
.) Let
K
be an object of

\mathscr{M}(S)
, and suppose that
K
is pure of some weight. Then we can write

K=\bigoplus_{Z}K_{Z}
, where the sum is over all integral closed subschemes

Z
of
S
, each
K_{Z}
is an object of
\mathscr{M}(S)
with strict support
Z
, and

K_{Z}=0
for all but finitely many
Z
.

Proof.

We prove the result by Noetherian induction on
S
. If

\dim S=0
, there is nothing to prove. Suppose that
\dim S\geq 1
,
and let
j:U\rightarrow S
be a nonempty open affine subset of
S
.
After shrinking
U
, we may assume that
U
is smooth and that
\mathsf{rat}^{\mathscr{M}}_{S}(K)[-\dim U]
is a locally constant sheaf on
U
.
Let
w
be the
weight of
K
. Then
[
Mor18
, Corollary 9.4]
implies that

j_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}K/j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}K
is of weight
\geq w+1
, so
the adjunction morphism

K\rightarrow j_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}K
factors through a morphism

K\rightarrow j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}K
. Similarly, the adjunction morphism

j_{!}^{\mathscr{M}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}K\rightarrow K
factors through a morphism

j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}\rightarrow K
. By definition of
j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}
, the composition

j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}K\rightarrow K\rightarrow j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}K
is the identity of

j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}K
. So we have
K=j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}K\oplus L
, with

j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}L=0
. The first summand has strict support
\overline{U}
by the remark
above, and
L_{\mid U}=0
, so the conclusion follows from the induction
hypothesis applied to
L_{\mid S-U}
.
∎

Theorem 6.24
.

Let
S
be as before, and let
w\in\mathbf{Z}
. Let
\mathscr{M}(S)_{w}
be
the full abelian subcategory of
\mathscr{M}(S)
whose objects
are motives that are pure of weight
w
.

Then
\mathscr{M}(S)_{w}
is semi-simple.

Proof.

By
6.23
,
we may assume that
S
is integral,
and it suffices to prove the result for the full subcategory
\mathscr{M}(S)_{w}^{0}
of
objects in
\mathscr{M}(S)_{w}
with strict support
S
itself.

Let
\eta
be the generic point of
S
. By

6.10
,
we have a full and essentially surjective exact functor
(given by the restriction morphisms)
2-\varinjlim_{U}\mathscr{M}(U)\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(\eta)
,
where the limit is over the projective system of nonempty affine open
subsets
U
of
S
. For such a
U
, we denote by

\mathscr{M}(U)_{w}^{0}
the full subcategory of
\mathscr{M}(U)
whose objects are
motives that are pure of weight
w
and have strict support
U
.
By Proposition
6.17
, the functor above induces a
full and essentially surjective functor

2-\varinjlim_{U}\mathscr{M}(U)_{w}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(\eta)_{w}
, and,
by
6.23
, this is turn gives a
full and essentially surjective functor

2-\varinjlim_{U}\mathscr{M}(U)^{0}_{w}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(\eta)_{w}
.
Moreover, if
j:U\rightarrow S
is a nonempty open subset, then the exact functor

j^{*}_{\mathscr{M}}:\mathscr{M}(S)^{0}_{w}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(U)_{w}^{0}
is an equivalence of categories, because
it has a quasi-inverse, given by
j_{!*}^{\mathscr{M}}
. So we deduce that the
restriction functor
\mathscr{M}(S)_{w}^{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(\eta)_{w}
is full and essentially
surjective. But this functor is also faithful, because the analogous functor
on categories of
\ell
-adic perverse sheaves is faithful. So

\mathscr{M}(S)^{0}_{w}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}(\eta)_{w}
is an equivalence of categories, which means that
we just need to show the theorem in the case
S=\eta
, i.e. if
S
is the
spectrum of a field.

Now suppose that
S=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}k
. Then, by
6.9
,

\mathscr{M}(k)_{w}=2-\varinjlim_{k^{\prime}}\mathscr{M}(k^{\prime})_{w}
,
where the limit is over all the subfields
k^{\prime}
of
k
that are
finitely generated over
\mathbf{Q}
. So it suffices to show the theorem for

k
finitely generated over
\mathbf{Q}
. But then we can embed
k
into
\mathbf{C}
,
and the conclusion follows from
[
HMS17
, Theorem 10.2.7]
.
∎

Definition 6.25
.

Let
K
be an object of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(X)
and
w\in\mathbf{Z}
. We say that
K
is
of weight
\leq w
(resp. of weight
\geq w
, resp. pure of weight

w
) if, for every
i\in\mathbf{Z}
, the perverse motive
\mathsf{H}^{i}K
is
of weight
\leq w+i
(resp. of weight
\geq w+i
, resp. pure of weight

w+i
).

Corollary 6.26
.

Let
K,L
be objects of
\mathscr{M}(S)
. If
K
and
L
are pure
of respective weights
i
and
j
, then
\Ext^{r}_{\mathscr{M}(S)}(A,B)=0

if
i<j+r
.

Proof.

By Lemma 4.5 of M. Saito’s
[
Sai91
]
,
this follows from the existence of the weight filtration and the
fact that it is strictly compatible with morphisms of
\mathscr{M}(S)
, and
from the semi-simplicity of pure objects of
\mathscr{M}(S)
.

∎

Corollary 6.27
.

(1)

There exists a unique weight structure (see
[
Bon14
, Definition 1.5]
) on
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(S)
whose heart is the full subcategory of complexes
of weight
0
.

(2)

Let
K,L
be objects of
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(S)
and
w\in\mathbf{Z}
.
If
K
is of
weight
\leq w
and
L
is of weight
>w
, then

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(S)}(K,L)=0
.

(3)

The weight structure of (1) is transversal to the
canonical
t
-structure on
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(S)
in the sense of
Definition 1.2.2 of Bondarko’s paper
[
Bon12
]
.

(4)

If
K\in\Ob\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(S)
is pure of some weight, then

K\simeq\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbf{Z}}\mathsf{H}^{i}K[-i]
.

Proof.

To prove (1), we apply part II of Theorem 4.3.2 of
[
Bon10
]
to
the triangulated category
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(S)
and the full subcategory
\mathscr{A}
of
complexes of weight
0
. This subcategory is stable by finite coproducts
and direct summands, and it generates
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(S)
. Indeed, to prove
the second statement, it suffices to show that the triangulated
subcategory generated by
\mathscr{A}
contains
\mathscr{P}(S)
; but every perverse motives
is a successive extension of pure perverse motives (by the existence of
the weight filtration), and, if
K
is a pure perverse motives, then
some shift of
K
is an
\mathscr{A}
. By Theorem 4.3.2 of
[
Bon10
]
,
there exists a weight structure on
\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(S)
with heart
\mathscr{A}
if and only if,
for every objects
K,L
of
\mathscr{A}
and every integer
n>0
, we have

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(S)}(K,L)=0
. As the functor
\mathrm{Hom}
is cohomological in
each variable, we may assume that
K
and
L
are concentrated in
one degree, so that there exist objects
A
and
B
that are pure
of respective weights
i
and
j
such that
K=A[-i]
and

L=B[-j]
. Then
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(S)}(K,L[n])=\Ext^{n+i-j}_{\mathscr{M}(S)}(A,B)

is zero by
6.26
.

We prove (2). We have
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(S)}(K,L)=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(S)}(K[-w],L[-w])
.
As
K[-w]
is of weight
\leq 0
and
L[-w]
is of weight
\geq 1
,
the statement follows from Proposition 1.3.3(1) of
[
Bon10
]
.

Point (3) follows immediately from the existence of the weight filtration on objects of
\mathscr{M}(S)
.

We prove(4). Let
w
be the weight of
K
.
Let
i\in\mathbf{Z}
. Then
\tau_{\leq i}K
and
\tau_{>i}K
are pure
of weight
w
, so
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}\mathscr{M}(S)}(\tau_{>i}K,\tau_{\leq i}K[1])=0
by
(3), so the exact triangle
\tau_{\leq i}K\rightarrow K\rightarrow\tau_{>i}K\xrightarrow{+1}

splits. This implies the statement.
∎

Theorem 6.28
.

Let
f:X\rightarrow S
be a proper morphism of quasi-projective
k
-varieties with
X
irreducible.
Let
j:U\rightarrow X
be an open immersion, and

K
be a perverse motive on
U
. If
K
is pure of weight
w
, then

\mathrm{H}^{i}(f_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}j^{\mathscr{M}}_{!*}K)
is a motivic perverse sheaf that is pure of weight
w+i
.

Proof.

Let us say that
L\in\mathrm{D}^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{M}(S))
is pure of weight
w
if
\mathrm{H}^{i}L
is pure of weight
w+i
for every
i\in\mathbf{Z}
. For such an
L
, by
6.12
, it follows from the Weil conjectures proved by Deligne in
[
Del80
]
that
f^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}L
is pure of weight
w
(see the remark after
[
Hub97
, Definition 3.3]
). Hence,
6.20
ensures that
f_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}j^{\mathscr{M}}_{!*}K
is pure of weight
w
. This gives the conclusion.
∎

In particular, this provides (for geometric variations of Hodge structures) an arithmetic proof of Zucker’s theorem
[
Zuc79
, Theorem p.416]
via reduction to positive characteristic and to the Weil conjectures
[
Del80
, Théorème 2]
. More generally, in higher dimension:

Corollary 6.29
.

Let
k
be a field embedded into
\mathbf{C}
.
Let
X
be an irreducible proper
k
-variety and
\mathscr{L}
be a
\mathbf{Q}
-local system on a smooth dense open subscheme
U
of
X
of the form
\mathscr{L}=R^{w}g_{*}\mathbf{Q}_{V}
where
g:V\rightarrow U
is a smooth proper morphism and
w\in\mathbf{Z}
is an integer. Then, the intersection cohomology group
IH^{i}(X,\mathscr{L})
, for
i\in\mathbf{Z}
, is canonically the Betti realization of a Nori motive over
k
which is pure of weight
i+w
. In particular,
IH^{i}(X,\mathscr{L})
carries a canonical pure Hodge structure of weight
i+w
.

Proof.

Let
d
be the dimension of
X
,
\pi:X\rightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(k)
be the structural morphism and
j
be the inclusion
of
U
in
X
.
As in
5.7
,
IH^{i}(X,\mathscr{L})
is the Betti realization of the Nori motive
\mathrm{H}^{i-d}(\pi_{*}^{\mathscr{M}}j^{\mathscr{M}}_{!*}\mathrm{H}^{w+d}(g^{\mathscr{M}}_{*}\mathbf{Q}^{\mathscr{M}}_{V}))
, which is pure of weight
w+i
by
6.28
.
∎
